| Exact context-indexed frames, uniform shorthand retained | Describes both calls and keeps old uniform certificates. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:

```text
frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
```

Append:

```text
Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
is admitted. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.

Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A standalone reference
record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
```

Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
and a width from another token budget at the same variant/control count.
Add a homogeneous two-call control accepted with old `frame`, and combine
one context with 4ta fragments. Finite output/resource arithmetic is
checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The sibling test was read, not run; its
current `Unsupported` expectation documents the gap, not a completed fix.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate-context repair**, coordinated with
[cwq](b-isa-spec-cwq.md) and [jw7](b-isa-spec-jw7.md). Keep v2v open until
the encoding and consumer tests are accepted. A 0.10 deferral must retain
this explicit blocker; it cannot restrict the admissible call graph.

---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

(args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11). **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding. Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers. ## Publication and combined schema decisions

“0.9 K repair” means the existing council-reviewed repair track, not
permission to alter a published semantic contract silently. `spec/README.md`
normally requires a full-version bump for semantic changes. Council must
choose that publication route and explicit subformat/ABI compatibility
rules. Every note's unchanged-source-hash statement assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged source envelope bytes. A version bump
changes all affected version-prefixed envelopes and consumer pins. The
ref catalog changes only if its own versioned bytes change; none of these
proposals needs a silent rewrite of `ref@0.9`.

Use one context vocabulary across v2v/cwq/jw7: `context_key` is exactly
§12.1's concrete invocation chain in the bound root realisation, including
instance, call_index, loop iterations and applicable oracle step/phase.
The root key is []; nested default-covered calls are included. Resolve
variant/controls/arguments/budget/placements from that tree. Order arrays
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of each key's JCS encoding. Frame/resource/
fault arrays validate the same context identities where applicable;
variant/control-count alone is never an invocation identifier. K12 exact
rationals and outward resource display remain unchanged. ysw separately
adds the missing token equality relation; it does not change params_hash
or serialize process ids.

Recommended acceptance groups are applicability/owner binding (0jq),
frames and reports (4ta/v2v/cwq/jw7), borrow proofs (7s0), token context
(ysw), and native models (95w). The fixture and migration dispositions
should proceed with their existing owners. K numbers are assigned only
at acceptance, avoiding collisions with concurrent council work.

  CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/.tmp/sol-contract-pass2/target
```

The environment above is a command prefix. Scratch and the required
single Resume block are under `.tmp/sol-contract-pass2/`; durable
deliverables are only this index and the 13 sibling notes.

The script additionally checks an in-memory body-order correction, writes
only its resulting fixture to job scratch, and compares both documents
after removing interface-enumeration differences (31 interfaces and 10
edge-interface arrays). Every other field,
including all 86 leaf digests and 16 cases, is identical. It independently
counts the 31 differing interfaces. The corrected fixture probe passes;
this does not edit or claim acceptance of the published fixture repair.
An initial combined run timed out at a 45-second per-probe debug limit
after the document/arithmetic/CLI checks passed. The final command uses
120 seconds per probe and 300 seconds overall; no assertion was removed.
Final document/arithmetic additions were rechecked with the same block's
prefix through the line before `def run(argv):`; the unchanged reference
and probe checks above were already complete.
The catalog/document-construction portion through the line before
`for path in (original_path, corrected_path):` was also re-executed:
31 differing interfaces, 10 differing edge arrays, and both artifact
digests below passed. These final checks used `timeout 30 python3 -I -B
-u -`, with the same extraction and `compile`/`exec` method and a distinct
`validation_source` variable so the executed document code could not
overwrite the extraction buffer.

sibling worktrees, contrary to this runner's explicit no-git/owned-path
rules. No source implementation changed, no Rust full test gate or sibling
gate is claimed, and no complete numerical certificate proof, new schema
implementation, council acceptance or full demo-rung run is claimed.

## Reproducible finite checks

From the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
timeout 300 python3 -I -B -u - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-pass2/INDEX.md')
s = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(s, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

This code checks exact small mathematical witnesses and actual published
reference interfaces. The arithmetic is not an implementation of any new
certificate verifier or a substitute for its required conformance tests.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from itertools import product
from fractions import Fraction as F
import copy

import json
import os
import re
import subprocess

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
OUT = ROOT / 'docs/notes/2026-11-02-pass2'

REF = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
PROBE = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/k82-probe'
assert Path.cwd() == ROOT
assert os.environ['TMPDIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'tmp')
assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))

isa = ['0jq', '4ta', 'v2v', '7s0', '95w', '0n5', 'jw7', 'cwq',
       'ysw', '552', 'ojy']
ids = ['b-isa-spec-' + x for x in isa] + [
       'b-dialect-spec-2cg', 'b-dialect-spec-djm']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {x + '.md' for x in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
for ident in ids:
    p = OUT / (ident + '.md')
    s = p.read_text()
    for title in ('Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                  'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes', 'Conformance', 'Release'):
        assert title.lower() in s.lower(), (ident, title)
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    # Remove fenced examples before treating Markdown syntax as links.
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    assert index.count('| [' + ident + ']') == 1, ident
print('PASS: 13 per-bead notes and index; required sections, fences and links')

# 0jq: H = M/sqrt(2), so M*M=2I exactly; each H column has two nonzeros.
M = ((1, 1), (1, -1))
assert [[sum(M[i][k]*M[k][j] for k in range(2)) for j in range(2)]
        for i in range(2)] == [[2, 0], [0, 2]]
assert all(sum(M[i][j] != 0 for i in range(2)) == 2 for j in range(2))
# 95w: Bell +/- have norm squared 2 before division by sqrt(2), overlap zero.
plus, minus = [1, 0, 0, 1], [1, 0, 0, -1]
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip(plus, minus)) == 0
assert sum(a*a for a in plus) == sum(a*a for a in minus) == 2
assert F(1)-F(1) == 0

# 4ta, v2v, cwq: exact basis maps and separate root/child footprints.
for t in range(4):
    assert (t ^ 3) ^ 3 == t
for t, u in product(range(2), repeat=2):
    assert ((t ^ 1) ^ 1, (u ^ 1) ^ 1) == (t, u)
assert (2, 1*2, 1*2, 1, 1) == (2, 2, 2, 1, 1)  # split time, A/B occupancy/peak
child_footprints = [(1, 1, 1), (1, 2, 2)]  # time, occupancy, peak
root = (2, 2*2, 2)
assert root == (2, 4, 2)
assert sum(c[2] for c in child_footprints) != root[2]
assert root[1] + sum(c[1] for c in child_footprints) != root[1]
assert F(1) + F(1,4) == F(5,4) and F(2) + F(1,4) == F(9,4)

# 7s0: a finite model of K76's all-operand stack rule for these CX-only prefixes.
def entry_c(sequence):
    values = {'a': None, 'x': None, 'c': 0}
    stacks = {w: [] for w in values}
    events = []
    for control, target in sequence:  # cx and mcx(1) already normalized to one kind
        identity = (control, target)
        operands = (control, target)
        top = stacks[control][-1] if stacks[control] else None
        if (top is not None and all(stacks[w] and stacks[w][-1] == top
                                   for w in operands)
                and events[top][0] == identity):
            before = events[top][1]
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].pop()
                values[w] = before[w]
        elif values[control] == 0:
            continue  # disabled: no causal event
        else:
            event = len(events)
            events.append((identity, {w: values[w] for w in operands}))
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].append(event)
            values[target] = (values[target] ^ values[control]
                              if all(values[w] is not None for w in operands)
                              else None)
    return values['c']
assert entry_c([]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('a','c')]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('x','c'), ('a','c'), ('x','c')]) is None
def frame_ok(c_values):
    return all((c, b ^ c) == (c, b) for c in c_values for b in range(2))
assert frame_ok([0]) and not frame_ok([0, 1])
assert not frame_ok(sorted(set([0] + [1])))  # repeated-visit D_F union cannot be narrowed
entry_products = [(0,0), (1,1)]
assert all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d in entry_products for b in range(2))
assert not all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d,b in product(range(2), repeat=3))
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip((1,1), (1,-1))) == 0  # phase on B changes |+>

# 0n5: legal state size versus sample count, without allocating it.
assert (1 << 63) // 8 == (1 << 60)
# jw7: all input/target/fault tuples; F_B's code, not final XOR word.
for budget, expected_faults in [(1, 256), (2, 0)]:
    faults = 0
    for x, target, fault in product(range(4), range(4), range(16)):
        payload, code = (0, 1) if budget == 1 else ((x+1) % 4, 0)
        after = (x, target ^ payload, fault ^ code)
        assert 0 <= after[1] < 4 and 0 <= after[2] < 16
        faults += code != 0
    assert faults == expected_faults
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0 and 1 ^ 0 == 1

# ysw: identity partitions are independent of canonical tuple values.
def partition(handles):
    seen, result = {}, []
    for h in handles:
        if h not in seen:
            seen[h] = len(seen)
        result.append(seen[h])
    return result
def valid_labels(labels):
    return (all(type(x) is int and x >= 0 for x in labels)
            and labels == partition(labels))
assert partition([10,20,20]) == [0,1,1]
assert partition([10,20,30]) == [0,1,2]
assert partition([20,20]) == [0,0] and partition([]) == []
assert all(not valid_labels(x) for x in ([1,1], [0,2], [0,-1]))
args = [['1','0','0','0'], ['1','0','0','0']]
def jcs(x):
    return json.dumps(x, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':'), ensure_ascii=False).encode()
params = hashlib.sha256(jcs(args)).hexdigest()
assert params == hashlib.sha256(jcs(copy.deepcopy(args))).hexdigest()
assert jcs({'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,0]}) != jcs(
           {'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,1]})
assert (4 if partition([20,20]) == [0,0] else 3) == 4
assert (4 if partition([20,30]) == [0,0] else 3) == 3

# 2cg: exact primitive request/bump history; production metadata stores are not simulated.
def allocation_trace(production, limit):
    bump, records = 4096, []
    def alloc(n):
        nonlocal bump
        if bump + max(n, 1) > 4096 + limit:
            raise OverflowError
        p = bump
        records.append((p, n))
        bump += max(n, 1)
        return p
    payload, code = None, 0
    try:
        alloc(3)
        if production:
            alloc(0)
        padding = (-bump) % 16
        if padding:
            alloc(padding)
        payload = alloc(16)
        if production:
            alloc(32)
    except OverflowError:
        code = 2
    return payload, bump, records, code
minimal = allocation_trace(False, 32)
production = allocation_trace(True, 64)
assert minimal == (4112, 4128, [(4096,3),(4099,13),(4112,16)], 0)
assert production == (4112, 4160,
                      [(4096,3),(4099,0),(4100,12),(4112,16),(4128,32)], 0)
failed = allocation_trace(True, 32)
assert failed == (4112, 4128, production[2][:-1], 2)
assert 16+32 == 32+16 == 48 and min(16,48) != min(32,48)
# djm: required public name/environment examples, not a musl process simulation.
assert '/bin/demo'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == 'demo'
assert '/bin/'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == ''
assert dict(s.split('=',1) for s in ['K=V'])['K'] == 'V'
print('PASS: structural, frame/resource, borrow, census, token, allocation and process witnesses')

def run(argv):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=ROOT, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=120)
    assert p.returncode == 0, (argv, p.returncode, p.stdout, p.stderr)
    return p.stdout
fixture = ROOT / 'conformance/fixtures/controlled-action-09/controlled.bisa'
pin = '45bbb506541e3a8bb28a18e1bb0aef16ba01ee1e7cac74d5a190deb3ad36ef5a'
for command in ('check', 'hash'):
    result = json.loads(run([REF, command, fixture]))
    assert result['status'] == 'ok' and result['object_hash'] == pin
for t, c in product(range(2), repeat=2):
    for initial, adjoint, expected in [(t, False, t ^ c), (t ^ c, True, t)]:
        cmd = [REF, 'run-action', fixture, 'controlled', '--mut', str(initial), '--in', str(c)]
        if adjoint:
            cmd += ['--adjoint']
        result = json.loads(run(cmd))
        assert result['mut'] == [expected] and result['in'] == [c]
        assert result['state'] == {str(expected)+str(c): [1,0]}
print('PASS: published B5/K13 check/hash identity and 4 forward/4 adjoint action cases')

# ojy: independent written-order sweep of the current 54 bare-formal recipes.
catalog_path = ROOT / 'spec/ref-0.9.json'
raw = catalog_path.read_bytes()
recipes = {r['kind']:r for r in json.loads(raw)['recipes']}
pin_ref = '5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197'
assert hashlib.sha256(b'isa=0.9\nexpansion=ref\nversion=0.9\n'+raw+b'\n').hexdigest() == pin_ref
body_interfaces = {}
for kind, recipe in recipes.items():
    if not all(isinstance(p,str) for p in recipe['parameters']):
        continue
    used = []
    def walk(body, bound):
        for node in body:
            if 'gate' in node:
                for wire in node['wires']:
                    name = wire.split('[',1)[0]
                    if name not in bound and name not in used:
                        used.append(name)
            elif 'alloc' in node:
                bound.add(node['alloc'])
            elif 'for' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound | {node['for']})
            elif 'inverse' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound)
            elif 'release' in node:
                assert node['release'] in bound
            else:
                raise AssertionError((kind, node))  # no unexamined constructors
    walk(recipe['body'], set(recipe['parameters']))
    body_interfaces[kind] = used
assert len(body_interfaces) == 54
changed = [k for k,v in body_interfaces.items() if v != sorted(v)]
assert len(changed) == 31
assert body_interfaces['value.select'] == ['p','b','a','t']

# Execute the existing generator only through document construction, before its file-write branch.
# The one-line correction is in memory; the published generator/fixture are never written.
generator = ROOT / 'conformance/fixtures/accepted-k82-09/generate.py'
source = generator.read_text().split('target = HERE / "k82.json"',1)[0]
assert source.count('return sorted(used)') == 1
scope = {'__file__':str(generator), '__name__':'pass2_in_memory_generator'}
exec(compile(source.replace('return sorted(used)', 'return used'), str(generator), 'exec'), scope)
corrected = scope['doc']
original_path = generator.parent / 'k82.json'
original = json.loads(original_path.read_text())
assert corrected['interfaces'] == body_interfaces
assert sum(original['interfaces'][k] != corrected['interfaces'][k]
           for k in body_interfaces) == 31
changed_edges = sum(a['interface'] != b['interface']
                    for a,b in zip(original['edges'], corrected['edges']))
assert len(original['edges']) == len(corrected['edges']) == 20
assert changed_edges == 10
without_enumeration = copy.deepcopy(corrected)
without_enumeration['interfaces'] = original['interfaces']
for old, new in zip(original['edges'], without_enumeration['edges']):
    new['interface'] = old['interface']
assert without_enumeration == original
assert sum(len(e['instances']) for e in corrected['edges']) == 86
assert len(corrected['cases']) == 16
corrected_path = SCRATCH / 'k82-body-order.json'
corrected_path.write_bytes(jcs(corrected) + b'\n')
for path in (original_path, corrected_path):
    output = run([PROBE, path, catalog_path])
    assert 'edges: 86 match, 0 differ' in output
    assert sum(': PASS' in line for line in output.splitlines()) == 16, output
print('PASS: K82 31/54 ordering mismatches;', changed_edges,
      'edge interface arrays differ; all other fields identical; both probes 86/86 and 16/16')
print('K82 original fixture SHA-256:', hashlib.sha256(original_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
print('K82 corrected scratch fixture SHA-256:', hashlib.sha256(corrected_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
print('PASS: pass2 verification complete; proposed schemas remain unimplemented/unaccepted')

## Remaining acceptance work

Council must decide and publish the exact repairs above before consumers
invent fields. Implementation owners then add the proposed positive and
negative vectors to suites C/F/H and relevant checker/producer tests.
552 needs actual producer adoption; ojy needs the fixture-owner repair;
the two dialect dispositions belong to that contract's council. Every
bead stays open for those decisions/work. This notes-only pass is complete
when the listed commands and finite checks pass; it does not close the
underlying contract or implementation obligations.
# v2v — frame descriptors for every realised invocation context

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-v2v` (P1).

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.1 “Invocation contexts” includes every reached invocation,
including defaults, with resolved arguments, budget and choices. §12.3
stores one region record per closure hash and requires one B5 frame.
§9.4 requires each slot's concrete target class and substitutes parameters
and budget into width constants before forming c-only expressions.
The same callee can receive A in one call and B in another; no single
descriptor can express both. A `(variant,controls)` key alone also fails
when those values agree. This is a C4/C5 contract defect.

North Star §§4.2/4.4/5.1 requires freely nested regions and independently
checkable schedules/certificates. Refusing repeated mixed-class calls
would narrow this machine. [4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) describes a different
case: a single slot fragmented within one context. Both repairs compose.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the source from `../bennett-sched/tests/pipeline.rs`, test
`context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported`:
root `pair(t,u)` calls the same one-bit `flip` on t and then u; flip is X.
Place t in A and u in B, capacity 1 each. Each call is forward with zero
inherited controls and the same closure hash. Callee frame one has
`mut:0,class:A,width:1`; callee frame two has B instead.

For all four root basis inputs, the result is `(t XOR 1,u XOR 1)`.
At unit duration/rate the root time is 2 and each root interface bit is
live for both slots: occupancy[A]=occupancy[B]=2, peaks 1 each.
The per-invocation callee views have duration 1 and just their respective
class. Neither copying A into both records nor taking a class wildcard
describes the realised placement. This witness needs no heterogeneous
slot and no nonzero epsilon.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Duplicate region records or rename the callee | Breaks closure-keyed identity or unnecessarily changes the source. |
| Use one wildcard/maximal frame | Cannot verify concrete classes and actual widths. |
| Exact context-indexed frames, uniform shorthand retained | Describes both calls and keeps old uniform certificates. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:

```text
frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
```

Append:

```text
Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
is admitted. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.

Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A standalone reference
record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
```

Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
and a width from another token budget at the same variant/control count.
Add a homogeneous two-call control accepted with old `frame`, and combine
one context with 4ta fragments. Finite output/resource arithmetic is
checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The sibling test was read, not run; its
current `Unsupported` expectation documents the gap, not a completed fix.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate-context repair**, coordinated with
[cwq](b-isa-spec-cwq.md) and [jw7](b-isa-spec-jw7.md). Keep v2v open until
the encoding and consumer tests are accepted. A 0.10 deferral must retain
this explicit blocker; it cannot restrict the admissible call graph.

 succeeded in 73ms:
# 4ta — one interface slot placed across several classes

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-4ta` (P1).

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §11 “Topology placement” supplies one vertex **per bit**, in
§9.4 slot order, and callees inherit actual operand placements. §8.3
assigns a class to each vertex. Neither clause requires all bits of a
source interface slot to have the same class. B5 (§9.4), however, gives
each slot exactly one `class` and its full signature `width`; unknown
fields and duplicate slot identities are not an extension mechanism
(§9.1). A legal mixed placement has no truthful descriptor. This is a
contract defect under C4/C5/C8, distinct from [v2v](b-isa-spec-v2v.md):
4ta concerns different bits **within one invocation of one slot**.

North Star §§4.1/4.3/4.4 promises virtual memory, allocation classes and
target-driven placement/resource reporting. Splitting a virtual slot
must not require a user rewrite or a target with a larger homogeneous
class. Class capacities still constrain physical feasibility; no capacity
is manufactured by this repair.

## Alternatives and worked witness

An action `split(t:wire[2] mut)` applies `x t[0]` and `x t[1]`.
A target has two vertices, 0:A and 1:B, each class capacity 1, and exact
single-wire X entries of duration 1 for either class. Placement
`interface_vertices:[[0,1]]` is legal: there is no multi-wire adjacency
requirement to satisfy. Both bits stay live for two sequential slots.
For unit class rates, time=2, peak[A]=peak[B]=1 and
qubit_second[A]=qubit_second[B]=2. On the four values t=0,1,2,3, the
outputs are respectively 3,2,1,0; the adjoint restores every input.

One B5 record `class:A,width:2` lies about bit 1 and exceeds A's capacity;
class B has the symmetric problem. Two `mut:0` records lose uniqueness;
two new source slots change the interface and its hashes unnecessarily.

| Alternative | Tradeoff |
|---|---|
| Ordered per-bit class vector | Precise, but repeats one class name per bit. |
| Ordered maximal class fragments | Precise, compact for runs, retains the source slot. Recommended. |
| Require a homogeneous class or split the source signature | Removes this legal placement or changes the program interface. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

In §9.4 replace the `slots` record production with:

```text
{id, role, width, class} |
{id, role, width, fragments:[{offset, width, class}, ...]}
```

Append:

```text
Each source slot still has exactly one record, its original id and role,
and its complete signature width. Exactly one of class and fragments
is present. class abbreviates one fragment at offset 0 covering width.
For the fragmented form, fragment offsets and widths are nonnegative
and positive integers respectively, using §9.1 integer encodings.
Fragments are in increasing offset order, start at 0, meet without gaps
or overlap, and end at the instantiated slot width. Each class is a
resolved declared target class. Adjacent equal-class fragments must be
merged; use the class form when there is only one fragment.

Flatten bundle bits LSB first, or region cells in address order and
each cell LSB first, exactly as for the existing slot. A fragment names
the class of that half-open bit interval; it is not a new source slot,
allocation, lifetime, alias or permission to share storage. Repeated
slot ids, wrong roles/widths, gaps, overlaps or out-of-range fragments
reject. Slot widths retain §8.1's c-only expression rules; fragment
widths/offsets describe the resolved fixed signature width.

With topology, every bit's fragment class must equal the class of its
§11 vertex; vertices and simultaneous live uses remain disjoint. A
callee's fragments must equal the inherited operand-class vector. In
the absence of topology, they bind that resolved vector without naming
vertices. Capacity, occupancy and peak accounting use each bit's actual
class, and charge no wire twice. Existing homogeneous records and
scratch/history/borrow/ancilla schemas retain their meanings.
```

The witness descriptor is one slot
`{id:"mut:0",role:"mut",width:2,fragments:[{offset:0,width:1,class:"A"},`
`{offset:1,width:1,class:"B"}]}`. This is proposed record notation;
the production's field names are exact. It is not accepted B5 today.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

The slot/signature/source closure stays the same. A repaired certificate
changes bytes; its existing placement schedule and target need not.
No reference recipe, leaf ordinal or catalog hash changes. Circuits,
scheduler, estimator and checker must validate fragments against the same
bit ordering and retain class-qualified resource rows. This does not
alter the explicit class of a source `qalloc` or add routing.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Test the four outputs and inverse above, both topology and class-only
descriptors, a region[1,2] slot with four flattened bits, and a homogeneous
legacy record. Reject a gap, overlap, width zero, split duplicate id,
incorrect class for a vertex, reversed offset order and adjacent unmerged
runs. Add a controlled invocation with the control charged separately,
not inserted into `mut:0`. The exact finite arithmetic is checked in
[INDEX](INDEX.md); target/scheduler implementation is not executed here.

Source context read: circuits' `docs/design/2026-11-01-m5-contract-requests.md`,
“Heterogeneous interface-slot frames”. Its named refusal is honest
incompleteness, not a new legality rule for source programs.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K/B5 encoding extension** alongside v2v; the gaps are
independent and both require vectors. Waiting for 0.10 leaves an explicit
blocker for complete 0.9 certificates, not permission to omit mixed
placements. Keep 4ta open for council and consumer work.
# cwq — resource contexts must select the same frame and invocation

Incremental proposal for `b-isa-spec-cwq`. The defect, alternatives,
flip witness and exact `resource_contexts` proposal already appear in
[the first-pass resource note](../2026-11-02-contract-checker-resource-contexts.md).
This note adds frame/class integration and attribution precision.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §8.2 defines accounting for an event list; §12.1 defines every
reached context; §12.3 publishes flat `resources`/`gates_used` without
selecting an invocation or defining an aggregation. An exact count for
one call is not a common resource bound like §12.3's error bounds.
This remains the same C4/C5 defect. North Star §§4.3/4.4/5.1 promises
the complete resource vector for general nested/controlled code; sums,
maxima or omission of contexts cannot silently replace that contract.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the first pass's worked a(t,c)/flip witness and alternatives, including
its root-versus-child totals. The added issue is **ownership charging**:
interface wires stay with the caller under §8.2; child views are
independently checkable invocation footprints, not extra charges.

Additional witness from [v2v](b-isa-spec-v2v.md): two forward, uncontrolled
flip contexts on A and B have identical time/count and different class
rows. A context key must choose both the right frame and resources.
[4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) additionally requires per-bit fragment classes
within one of those frames. A variant/control-only resource key cannot
resolve either difference in general.

A new attribution witness: one wire is live over [0,2), with owner rate 1
and borrowed over [0,1) at dirty_rate=1/4. Its combined occupancy charge
is 1 + 1/4 = 5/4. Keeping the owner's full charge and then adding the
borrower yields 2 + 1/4 = 9/4, which violates §8.2's replacement rule.
These are declared accounting intervals, not an unreported circuit run.

| Alternative | Result |
|---|---|
| Sum/max flat maps or choose a convenient context | Already rejected by the first pass; exact replay remains ambiguous. |
| Per-context reports with a deterministic legacy projection | Keep the first-pass recommendation. |
| Add child reports as costs to the root | Double counting; contradicts leaf-once aggregation. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Apply the first-pass `resource_contexts:[{context_key,resources,
gates_used,estimate?}]` and first-canonical-context projection. Append:

```text
Resolve every resource_contexts entry from exactly the invocation tree
used for §12.1 claims and frame_contexts. Its key selects that context's
resolved frame, placements, borrowed lifetimes, controls, budget and
choices before any accounting. Class-fragment slots are expanded to
their actual bit classes. Validate resources for all declared target
classes, including zero-valued unused class rows where K12 applies;
never merge class rows into a replacement scalar.

Each context report describes its invocation subtree's event stream
and wire footprint. At a call boundary, interface wires remain charged
to their caller under §8.2; displaying them in a child invocation view
does not create a second billable allocation or event. Borrow charges
replace the owner's charge during the borrower interval as specified
there. Root totals are recomputed from the root event/live-interval
stream once. Neither resources nor gates_used are obtained by adding
the root view to its overlapping child views. Inclusive error-bound
multiplicities do not define a resource aggregation rule.

Missing, foreign or mismatched frame/resource contexts reject the
asserted report. Different record arrays must not be independently
zipped by position as an alternative to comparing their context keys.
```

This makes the first-pass “callee views” precise while retaining caller
ownership, borrowed-rate replacement and exact K12 outward display rules.
It introduces no new optimizer objective, routing or physical model.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

The certificate/report changes bytes; source, target, schedule and ref
catalog need not. New model/alias/target changes are separate dependencies,
not inherent resource-context hash changes. Scheduler, estimator and
checker must retain identical keys and class rows, and must not copy an
estimate from the first context to another with equal variant/controls.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Reuse first-pass flip/root values; add A/B callee frames, a fragmented
slot, an idle class, a dirty borrow interval with owner-charge replacement,
and different budgets under one variant/control key. Reject swapped keys,
missing default-covered call, copied first-context estimate, or root-plus-
children totals. Exact footprint/root arithmetic is checked in
[INDEX](INDEX.md); no new scheduler/checker or sibling gate was run.
Origin read: checker `.tmp/CROSS_REPO_BEADS.md`, item 4, and sched's
two-class test named in v2v.

## Release and route

Recommend the **existing 0.9 K resource-context repair**, with v2v/4ta
and the shared context convention accepted together. Keep cwq open;
this note adds integration requirements, not another competing schema.
# jw7 — fault-rate binding, coordinated with applicability and context

Incremental proposal for `b-isa-spec-jw7`. Incorporate
[the first-pass fault-rate derivation](../2026-11-02-contract-checker-fault-rate.md),
including its two-budget witness and explicit standalone/context schemas.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.3's `fault_rate` has a domain and counts but no invocation;
claims in the same record may bind different arguments/budgets by K13.
§12.1 also reaches multiple scheduled contexts. Which F_B is recounted
is therefore undefined. This remains the same C5 contract gap. North
Star §§5.1/8 requires the reported rate for the actual declared acceptance
invocation; it cannot be dropped or guessed from a convenient claim.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the first pass's fully worked B=1/B=2 oracle census and incoming-XOR
counterexample. Its explicit-binding recommendation and rejected
inheritance/one-claim alternatives stand; no competing encoding is added.

The missing second-pass interaction is [0jq](b-isa-spec-0jq.md): an
action's structural-only packet should have no own fault census. Making
the field optional must not make an oracle's requested census optional
to verify. [v2v](b-isa-spec-v2v.md)'s frame selector must resolve the same
invocation as this census.

## Recommended exact spec diff

Apply the first-pass `fault_rate.invocation` and `fault_rate_contexts`
text. Add to §12.3 immediately after the applicability repair:

```text
When a fault census is asserted for an oracle record, every supplied
rate binds its own K13 invocation and full §12.1 context where scheduled.
For a multi-context scheduled oracle, fault_rate_contexts covers every
reached invocation once in JCS context-key order; fault_rate is the
first-context projection specified above. Frame, resource and rate
arrays use the same bound invocation tree and context keys. A default-
covered invocation is not omitted because no schedule entry names it.

An action has no F_B outcome of its own. Absence of its fault_rate
is not a statement about nested oracles. Their rates, if asserted, are
recorded under their own closure/context identities. A structural
adjoint or borrow proof is not a fault census. Supplied counts must
still be independently recounted; absence cannot discharge a requested
zero-fault algorithm/rung acceptance obligation.

If token identity context is adopted, the bound invocation includes its
canonical alias relation wherever applicable; equal tuple encodings do
not silently substitute a different identity-dependent context.
```

The last paragraph depends on [ysw](b-isa-spec-ysw.md) acceptance; it is
not an unannounced change to current K13 bytes. For unbound legacy
reports, the first pass's unchecked census outcome remains honest
incompleteness, not approval of a complete executable certificate.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

As in the first pass, certificate bytes change; existing source, closure,
target, schedule and ref catalog bytes do not change from this repair
alone. If ysw adds alias bindings, affected schedules also change under
that separate repair. Checkers, circuits, scheduler and rung harness must
select the identical invocation and preserve justified exclusion classes.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Reuse the full 256-tuple two-budget vectors and first-pass binding
negatives. Add a structural-only action with no rate, two nested oracle
contexts at B=1 and B=2, reordered claims, a default-only invocation, and
a census keyed to the other context's frame. Require independent counts
and reject a supplied conflicting invocation. The exact count/XOR
arithmetic is checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md); no new reference census or
checker implementation is claimed. Origin read: checker
`.tmp/CROSS_REPO_BEADS.md`, item 3.

## Release and route

Recommend the **existing 0.9 K fault-binding repair**, accepted with the
0jq applicability and common context rules. Keep jw7 open for that work;
do not duplicate the first-pass proposal or weaken fault-rate acceptance.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

This **aggregation witness** assumes the displayed scheduled intervals,
unit class rates and no other events; it does not assert an unbound
oracle gate count. A permitted cost expression `1 / 3` produces that
makespan. The exact class peaks are 2 and 1, not a replacement scalar 3;
occupancies are 2/3 and 1/3, and the idle class is present. Changing the
argument `2` to `1` denotes a different request, even at the same budget.
No 0.8 request can transmit that distinction through §10's signature.

## 11. Scheduling interface (sched 0.9)

```c
typedef struct bennett_sched_request_t bennett_sched_request_t;   typedef struct bennett_sched_response_t bennett_sched_response_t;
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_new(bennett_sched_request_t** out);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_object(bennett_sched_request_t*, const uint8_t*, size_t);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_target(bennett_sched_request_t*, const uint8_t* jcs, size_t);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_region(bennett_sched_request_t*, const char* closure_hash_hex, const char* variant, uint32_t inherited_controls);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_budget(bennett_sched_request_t*, uint64_t steps, uint64_t heap, uint64_t stack, uint64_t depth);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_params(bennett_sched_request_t*, const uint8_t* params_jcs, size_t);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_accuracy(bennett_sched_request_t*, const char* epsilon_decimal);
bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_capacity(bennett_sched_request_t*, const char* cls, uint64_t peak_qubits);
void           bennett_sched_request_free(bennett_sched_request_t*);
bennett_status bennett_sched_schedule(const bennett_sched_request_t*, bennett_sched_response_t** out);
typedef enum { BENNETT_SCHED_OK, BENNETT_SCHED_INFEASIBLE, BENNETT_SCHED_UNSUPPORTED } bennett_sched_outcome;
bennett_sched_outcome bennett_sched_response_outcome(const bennett_sched_response_t*);
const char*    bennett_sched_response_reason(const bennett_sched_response_t*);
const uint8_t* bennett_sched_response_schedule(const bennett_sched_response_t*, size_t* len);
const uint8_t* bennett_sched_response_certificate(const bennett_sched_response_t*, size_t* len);
void           bennett_sched_response_free(bennett_sched_response_t*);
```

**Generated operations and the expansion library.** Every row and
instruction expands into **generated operations** (gates, allocations,
releases). Above gate level the order is fixed by §3.6, §4.2, §4.5–§4.6
and §5.2 (dispatch comparators k = 0..n in index order; a row's
sub-operations in listed order; successor XORs; commit; cleanup). Below
that level (the gate decomposition of comparators, adders, multiplexers,
`inrange` scans and their scratch) the expansion is defined by an
**expansion library**: a versioned document, identified in the
schedule's `bindings.expansion` as `{ "name", "version", "hash" }`, that
lists for every row kind and every instruction its generated operations
in order, with their **ordinals**, scratch identities and the subset it
marks removable, and that is extensionally equal to the reference (§5.7,
T6). A consumer that does not implement the bound library rejects the
schedule (`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE`); two consumers implementing it
execute and charge the same operation set. `bennett-isa-spec` publishes
the reference library `ref` (§17 item 7). §5.7 transformations under a
schedule MUST keep the ordinals of the operations they keep and MAY
remove only operations the library marks removable.

**Schedule document** (JCS) has the following schema, including reserved
support points and optional instance/call-occurrence selectors. [deps: ISA-11-P2, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P22, ISA-11.1-P29]

```
  { "bindings": { "closure_hash", "target_hash", "region", "variant", "inherited_controls", "budget": [B,heap,stack,depth],
                  "params_hash", "epsilon", "expansion": { "name", "version", "hash" }, "scheduler": { "name", "version" } },
    "interface_vertices"?: [ [v, …], … ],
    "entries": [ { "path": [ { "symbol": hex, "point": "<block>:<ordinal>" | "$construct" | "$step" | "$commit" | "$release", "iteration": [n, …] | "*",
                              "instance"?: [n, …] | "*", "call_index"?: n | "*" }, … ],
                   "step": n | "*",  "phase": "fwd" | "rev" | "*",  "generated": n | "*",
                   "kind": "cleanup" | "gate" | "alloc" | "start",
                   "cleanup": { "realisation": "reverse" | "recompute" },
                   "gate":    { "realisation": "native" | "<scheme name>", "epsilon_share": decimal },
                   "alloc":   { "class": "<cls>", "vertices"?: [v, …] },           /* a concrete class; "any" is not allowed in a schedule */
                   "start":   { "time": decimal } } ] }
```

**Topology placement.** With a §8.3 topology, `interface_vertices`
lists inherited control wires first as individual width-one arrays, then
one vertex array per source interface slot in §9.4 slot order; array
elements follow bit order (and region cell address then bit order). A callee inherits its actual
operand placements. `alloc.vertices`, when present, assigns every bit
of that allocation in bit order. These arrays MUST have the declared
width, resolve to the correct classes and have disjoint live uses;
without a topology they MUST be absent. Omitted placements use the
lowest-index free vertex of the resolved class, assigning bits in order;
interface slots are assigned first in slot order, then allocations in
generated order. Vertices are retained for a wire's lifetime and freed
on release; borrowed wires retain their owner's vertices. Every native
leaf, including scheme leaves, MUST satisfy §8.3 adjacency, else
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` naming §17 item 15. No routing is inserted.

**Matching.** Every generated operation has an **invocation chain**:
the program points of the region calls and `lift.oracle_call`s that led
to it, outermost first, ending in its own point. [deps: ISA-4.7, ISA-4.2, ISA-3.6]
`lift.call` adds no element: an lproc row in an oracle's closure generates
its expansion once per fixed row instance per step under its dispatch
predicate, independently of which `lift.call` reached that instance or
the recursion depth. [deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6]
`path` matches
an operation iff it is a **prefix** of that chain, element by element:
`symbol` and `point` equal, and `iteration` either `*` or equal to the
vector of 0-based dynamic repetition counts of the `qloop`s enclosing
that point (outermost first; its length is the point's loop nesting
depth, `[]` outside every loop); `instance` and `call_index` match as
specified below. [deps: ISA-4.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9]
An oracle invocation's forward step coordinate is t ∈ 1..B and its
inverse step t has `phase: rev`; the root B is `bindings.budget[0]`
and a nested oracle's B is the steps component of its own call token,
not the parent's B. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.4, ISA-10]
An entry's `step` and `phase` read the oracle invocation entered by the
last oracle-call element of its `path`, or the root invocation if there
is no such element. [deps: ISA-4.2, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.2-P3]
A call element enters that invocation only when it is followed along
the operation's chain into the callee: when the element is the
operation's own final point, it remains in its containing invocation. [deps: ISA-4.2, ISA-4.7]
`lift.call` never changes this level; a path ending at a call element
that is a proper prefix of the operation's chain already selects the
entered callee level. [deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-3.7, ISA-4.7]
At a level with no oracle step, including an action root and oracle
construction/commit/release outside the steps, only `step: "*"` and
`phase: "*"` match; an action's adjoint does not itself supply a
`rev` phase. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.3, ISA-4.7]
`generated` is the ordinal within the row's expansion in the bound
library, or within its reserved support pseudo-point (K64), or the
non-emitting cleanup carrier ordinal (K65). [deps: ISA-11-P2.S3, ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P29, ISA-6.4]

**Specificity** is the lexicographic tuple (path length, number of
non-`*` iterations, step non-`*`, phase non-`*`, generated non-`*`);
an entry applies iff all its selectors match, and among matching entries
of the same kind the unique maximal one applies. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-4.6]
Two distinct entries (distinct array positions, even if identical)
with equal maximal specificity for the same generated operation and
kind make a tie; this is the meaning of "incomparable", and document
order is never significant. [deps: ISA-11-P6.S1]
A tie makes the schedule invalid for that operation under the validation
stages below; overlapping equal-specificity entries do not make a tie
where a more-specific matching entry applies, including coverage by a
union of such entries. [deps: ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P7]
Operations outside an invocation's own budget or inside a zero-trip
loop are not generated and cannot witness a tie. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-4.6]
Selectors are resolved independently per `kind`.
A `gate` entry matches only gate operations, an `alloc` entry only
allocations, and a `start` entry every generated operation. [deps: ISA-11-P2.S1, ISA-11-P3]
The applying entry supplies the decision: an omitted field with a
default takes that default, never a value from a less-specific matching
entry. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-17]
Defaults
for uncovered operations: `reverse`; `native` if the gate matches a
`gates` entry, else the first matching scheme; class = the first class
declared in the target's `classes` object (JCS order) that has capacity
for it; sequential timing in generated order.
An operation uncovered for one kind, including in a nested invocation,
takes that kind's default directly, without inserting an entry or
altering any other operation's selected decision. [deps: ISA-8.3, ISA-17]
There is no requirement that these defaults be expressible by additional
entries in the single-`step`, single-`phase` selector language. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-17]
**Timing**: a `start` time
MUST be ≥ the end of every operation the operation depends on (data,
wire lifetime, region cell version, effect order); an entry violating
this makes the schedule invalid. **Obligations**: satisfy §6; respect
capacity; report every resource; be reproducible from the request plus
scheduler version.

**Validation stages (K55).** A consumer validates every binding and
every path against the object, including the maximal-match condition
at the stages specified here, and executes verbatim or rejects with
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE`. [deps: ISA-9.2, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P6.S3, ISA-11-P6.S4, ISA-11-P7.S2, ISA-11-P7.S3, ISA-11-P7.S7]
For admission, a nested invocation budget is **object-derivable** exactly
when substituting literal integer constants and signature-positional
classical/token forwarding into the four operands of `budget.mint`
yields one tuple for that call site; an unresolved root parameter,
conflicting tuples or any other unresolved operand makes it
non-derivable. [deps: ISA-4.1, ISA-3.7, ISA-5.4, ISA-10]
Literal propagation initialises every nonliteral value to unknown,
takes exact bit-vector values from `const.iN`, copies values through
positional call/return and branch/block-parameter bindings to a fixed
point, and joins all syntactic incoming values as unknown unless they
are all the same known value; arithmetic, predicate reasoning and
additional implementation-specific inference do not make a budget
object-derivable for this staging rule. [deps: ISA-4.1, ISA-3.4, ISA-3.7, ISA-5.4]
The root region's token parameters remain unresolved at object
admission even if a CC/R caller in the object mints a literal-budget
token, since that caller does not bind every invocation of the
region. [deps: ISA-4.1, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.4, ISA-10]
This derivation does not invert `params_hash`, assume a token id's
tuple, or substitute an enclosing invocation's budget. [deps: ISA-9.2, ISA-12.3, ISA-5.4]
A consumer MUST reject root-level ties and nested ties whose invocation
domains are object-derivable at admission, and MUST reject a tie in a
non-derivable nested domain at the first concrete operation in generated
order having multiple maximal matching entries, before applying any realisation to
that operation, with `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` and the diagnostic
"§11: multiple maximal matching schedule entries". [deps: ISA-11-P5, ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P6.S3, ISA-11-P7.S2, ISA-11-P7.S3, ISA-11-P7.S7, ISA-5.4, ISA-10]
A nested domain is object-derivable only if every oracle budget on its
invocation chain is object-derivable (the root uses the bound budget);
admission quantifies over the generated operations of these domains,
and defers the remaining domains without rejecting merely because
some possible budget would contain a tie. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-4.6, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P7.S2, ISA-11-P7.S3]
Admission success therefore leaves the prescribed concrete checks in
non-derivable domains outstanding, even when actual token values become
available earlier; learning them does not move rejection to a different
stage. [deps: ISA-5.4, ISA-10, ISA-11-P7.S2, ISA-11-P7.S6, ISA-11-P7.S7]
Schedule decimal numbers and decimal strings denote the exact rational
of their decimal text; equal values match and resolve identically
regardless of spelling. [deps: ISA-8.1-P1, ISA-5.2-P1]
Token parameters in `params_hash` use §12.3's canonical four-component
tuple encoding, for host- and device-minted tokens alike. [deps: ISA-12.3, ISA-5.4]

**Selection witnesses** *(non-normative)*. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-5.2-P3] For root B=2 and nested B=3,
nested step 3 exists; with nested B=2 it does not, independently of root
B. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.4] A path of `[]` with `step: 1` reads root step 1 even for an operation
at nested step 3; a path through the nested call reads nested step 3. [deps: ISA-4.2, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.2-P3]
Two `lift.call` sites p1/p2 into the same lproc row q give one row-q
expansion per fixed instance per enclosing step, with neither p1 nor p2
in its chain; different binding keys yield distinct instance components. [deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6]
An action-root X has no step/phase, including under adjoint. [deps: ISA-5.3, ISA-4.7]
Equal maximal entries A/B reject; A/B fully covered by higher C or a
union C1/C2 admit in every order; duplicate C entries reject. [deps: ISA-11-P3]
An alloc entry cannot shadow a gate entry. [deps: ISA-11-P2.S1, ISA-11-P3]
A more-specific realisation-only entry takes the default share even if
a broad entry writes a share. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-17]
For root-step-1 scheme X and an uncovered root-step-2/nested-step-1
gate, only the latter takes its default; no synthetic nested entry
overrides the former. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-17]
An unknown nested budget permits admission, but if the first ambiguous
operation is at nested step 3, B=3 rejects there before its realisation,
whereas B=2 has no such operation; an object-derivable B=3 rejects at
admission. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-5.4, ISA-10]
Numeral 0 and strings `"0"`, `"0.0"`, `"0.000"` all denote zero. [deps: ISA-8.1-P1, ISA-5.2-P1]

**Invocation-edge identity (K63).** Every invocation edge identifies the
source call point, the containing fixed-binding instance (the §3.7 integer
binding key, or `[]` outside an lproc instance), and the zero-based
semantic-call occurrence within that source row expansion, serialized as
`instance` and `call_index` alongside `symbol`, `point`, `iteration`.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-9.2-P1.S3, ISA-11.1-P9]
Number semantic calls in forward instantiated constructor traversal order
before descending into callees, counting `call` constructors rather than
`invoke`/`apply` recipe composition, independently of leaf ordinals;
inverse traversal retains forward occurrence numbers. [deps: ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P29.S3]
The operation's terminal point also carries its containing `instance`,
but has no `call_index` unless it is an invocation edge into a callee.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11.1-P9]
In schedule path elements each new selector may be `*`, and omission
means `*`; a concrete `instance` matches that exact integer array, and
a concrete `call_index` matches only an edge with that occurrence number.
[deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-11.1-P9]
Validate concrete instance keys against §3.7 source instance formation
and concrete call indices against the bound-library expansion at that
source point and instance, including static repetitions. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P9]
These selectors do not add specificity components: the existing
lexicographic tuple is unchanged, so equally specific overlapping
entries still tie unless covered by a more-specific match. [deps: ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S2, ISA-11-P6.S3]
The edge's source point selects the entered invocation for K52 exactly
as before; adding instance and call occurrence does not introduce a
`lift.call` invocation or another step level. [deps: ISA-11-P5.S5, ISA-11-P5.S6, ISA-11-P5.S7, ISA-3.7-P6]

**Non-row addresses (K64).** Non-row support leaves have reserved
pseudo-points `$construct`, `$step`, `$commit`, `$release` in their
containing oracle; these are legal path points rather than user block
names. [deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P22]
For the bound `oracle.construct` recipe, its initial `visit State`
subtree has point `$construct`, each forward or inverse `step` invocation
has point `$step`, the commit segment from allocation of `halt` through
release of `halt` has point `$commit`, and the final inverse `visit State`
subtree has point `$release`. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11.1-P22.S14, ISA-11.1-P29]
Within `step`, dispatch comparisons, HALT successor support, pc/next/frame
exchanges and dispatch cleanup stay at `$step`; `Rows[k]` and `Next[k]`
source-template subtrees instead retain source row k's point and shared
row ordinal space in traversal order, and semantic callees retain their
own invocation chains and spaces. [deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-11.1-P14, ISA-11.1-P22.S14, ISA-11.1-P29]
All nested helper `invoke`, loops and descriptor visits inherit their
containing point unless they enter one of those source-row or semantic
callee subtrees. [deps: ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P18, ISA-11.1-P22]
Number support leaves from zero in instantiated expansion order within
each pseudo-point, separately for each oracle step and phase at `$step`,
excluding all source-row and callee subtrees; inverse traversal retains
the corresponding forward step's ordinals and occurrence numbers. [deps: ISA-11.1-P29.S1, ISA-11.1-P29.S2, ISA-11.1-P29.S3, ISA-11.1-P9]
Reversing a complete already expanded construction retains each leaf's

 succeeded in 0ms:
an action region[63,1] with `cells: "all"` and `count: 1` remains legal but has
2^63 bits per element, so an unable host reports unchecked (host limit).
[deps: ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-12.1-P2, ISA-12.2-P1]

### 12.3 Schema and composition

```
  versions; bindings { object_hash, interface_hash, closure_hash (required), reference_program_hash, dialect_mapping_version, target_hash, schedule_hash?, invocation? (K13) }
  producer { name, version, materialisation { llvm_version, pipeline, normaliser_version } }
  regions { <closure_hash>: { kind, signature, bounds, estimate? (K12), frame (required typed-record descriptor, §9.4), resources { name: decimal }, gates_used { gate: { count, scheme?, epsilon? } },
            claims: [ <claim> … ], verification: { oracle: i, borrow_frames: [i], inverse_pairs: [ { g, ginv, claim: i, budget: [B,heap,stack,depth], arg_tokens?: { "<arg>": [B,heap,stack,depth] } } ], unitary: [i], channel: [i],
                                                   fault_rate: { domain, faults_by_code, undefined_excluded, layout_dependent_excluded } },
            cleanup: { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": [ { path, realisation } ] },
            error:   { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": { local: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v }, inclusive: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v } } },
            definedness_assumptions: [ids] } }
  composition { multiplicities: { "<hash>:<variant>:<inherited_controls>": n (from the realised schedule) }, epsilon_total: { "diamond": v }, method: "sum_over_multiplicities" }
```

**Invocation binding (K13).** An invocation is
`{ args: [value, …], budget: [B,heap,stack,depth] }`, with every value
resolved before replay and all four budget components explicit even
when zero. `args` is in signature order: `iN` and `ptr` values are
canonical **unsigned decimal strings** of exactly that bit-vector
value (ptr has `ptr_width` bits); `f64` is `0x` followed by exactly 16
lowercase hex digits of its IEEE 754 binary64 bit pattern, most
significant nibble first (so −0 and NaN payloads are not lost); a token
is its four-component tuple of canonical nonnegative decimal strings,
not a process-local token id. Budget components are also canonical
nonnegative decimal strings. Signature types disambiguate encodings;
widths/ranges and every tuple MUST pass §5.4 validation. `params_hash`
is SHA-256 of the UTF-8 JCS **args array**, no trailing LF (§9.2), not
of the whole invocation. Thus equal token tuples have equal parameter
hashes regardless of ids, while +0 and −0 `f64` parameters differ.

Each standalone table/program/structural claim MUST carry its own
`invocation`; it binds
**every** classical argument and budget. Missing values have no implicit
zero/default. Claims with no arguments still bind `args: []` and their
budget. K4 inverse-pair claims instead bind the entry's budget,
`arg_tokens` and quantified `args` domain as already specified; this
rule does not replace that quantification by one chosen argument.
A parameter-uniform structural claim MAY instead bind
`invocation: { args: "all", budget: "all" }`: its checker MUST discharge
the rule's side conditions for every well-typed argument vector and
valid budget, not by replaying a chosen instance. Thus generic library
and adjoint/translation proofs retain their universal scope.
A scheduled certificate MUST carry the root `bindings.invocation`;
its args hash and budget MUST equal the root schedule's bindings, and
all nested arguments and budgets MUST be resolved at each realised
invocation context (§12.1), including default-covered contexts.
Explicit nested bindings, if supplied, MUST equal those resolutions;
a root binding MUST NOT be silently reused for a different callee.
A checker MUST reject absent or conflicting bindings before replay,
and an executor may use a verified result only for the invocation(s)
it actually covers. Reference table/program/structural claims need no
schedule; they prove only their reference relation, not a target
realisation. Scheduled/numerical/channel claims retain the expansion
library gate and all existing context and composition obligations.

**CLAIM-BINDING witness**

```json
{"signature":["i128","f64","token"],"args":["18446744073709551617","0x8000000000000000",["2","0","0","0"]],"params_hash":"9f628a694fa7259bf899bfb677aabce1c084e268a738a40f7f643eb6d8c9c093"}
```

The first argument is > 2^64; the second is binary64 negative zero;
the third is a token tuple, not an id. Replacing negative zero with
`0x0000000000000000` changes `params_hash`; replacing the token by
another valid id of the same tuple does not.

`versions` is the object `{ "isa", "object", "launch", "sched", "cert",
"coverage", "target" }` of the seven header versions. A **variant** is a
nonempty string chosen by the producer (the ISA enumerates none); it is
compared bytewise.

`inclusive(R, key) = local(R, key) + Σ_callees n_c × inclusive(c, key_c)`
**per metric**, keyed like `cleanup` by `<variant>:<inherited_controls>`
(the realised error depends on both); every `local` value is a verified
common upper bound over all invocation contexts of its key in the bound
root schedule (§12.1), so multiplying by n_c is sound; an
`operator_norm` value converts
to `diamond` by ×2 once, at the point it is first combined with a
`diamond` value; `operator_norm` conclusions (T6, controlled unitary
variants) require `operator_norm` bounds throughout. A controlled
unitary variant requires a `numerical` claim; an ε > 0 realisation
compared as a channel requires a `channel` claim. Executable claims
(`numerical`, `channel`, and `exhaustive` or `sampled` with
`expected: "oracle"`, plus table/program comparisons of a realised
operation) bind to `schedule_hash` through their subject. Standalone
reference relations have only the invocation scope defined above (K13).

## 13. Coverage (coverage 0.9)

`{ "entries": [ { "point": <program-point id>, "source": "<dialect-spec stable id>", "classification": "lowered" | "trapped" | "shimmed", "reason", "fault_code"?, "shim_hash"?, "count" } ] }`.
`fault_code` is present iff `classification` is `trapped`, `shim_hash`
iff it is `shimmed`.

## 14. Formal model

### 14.1 Reference interpreter *(normative)*

Executes an oracle's reference body per basis configuration: locations
and cells are bit-vectors of their declared widths (no upper bound,
§3.3), pseudo-locations read-only, predication is
`if`, each §4.2 row is its classical function (including the inherent
`inrange` check of loads and stores, the activation records of §3.7, the
`alloctab` and stack arrays), `fault` and a triggered `lift.faultif`
record their effective cause (§5.4) and halt, `lift.
retval` halts with success and `f(x) = result_val`, the step counter
counts executed rows (on budget exhaustion exactly B rows have
executed), counters are unbounded integers. It **defines** F_B
and the budget unit and rejects any body that executes a non-permutation
gate.

For CC/R, `fault C` has the terminal-record semantics of §4.9 and the
logical history of §7.4: it preserves the complete executed prefix for
eligible reversal, including activations that have not returned. This
is T4's history traversal, not the Q machine's B inverse steps. In an
oracle body or lproc the same spelling remains the §4.2 fault row;
§3.6 and §5.2 supply its step-machine inverse and T2 prefix reversal.

### 14.2 Quantum state semantics

The state of a Q region is a vector in ⊗ over live wires in allocation
order (bundles little-endian). `qalloc` tensors |0⟩; `qfree` removes
clean factors; `qborrow` introduces a factor in unknown state possibly
entangled with a reference system R, and the region's operation must be
U ⊗ I on it (§5.6); gates act by §4.4 under the active predicate;
`measure` (CC) is the projective computational-basis measurement with
Born probabilities, consuming the wires.

### 14.3 Theorems

- **T1 (totality).** For every well-formed oracle, budget and x ∈ D the
  reference interpreter halts within B + 1 rows and F_B(x) is unique.
- **T2 (prefix reversal).** For every executor whose realisation is a
  composition of ε = 0 permutation gates implementing §5.2 steps 1–4
  with the inverse step of §3.6, on every x ∈ D every non-interface wire
  is clean after step 4 and borrowed bundles hold their initial values.
- **T3 (permutation theorem).** If the realised unitary V of a circuit on
  span(D) is a permutation matrix and the final basis tuple equals P(x)
  for every x ∈ D, then V|span(D) = P.
- **T4 (round trip).** For every finite CC/R trace with retained run
  history and tapes that is eligible for reversal under §10 — a
  `COMPLETED` or `FAILED` run containing no `measure` frame and no
  `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified — the history
  traversal of §7.4 restores the active environment, memory, allocation
  table, device-model state, the ledger entries of every register the
  run created or touched, token validity and active history (§7.4) to
  their entry values; audit records may remain; observed external
  events are not retracted. If, in addition, every region call is undone
  by the exact inverse of its realised forward operation (ε = 0
  realisations, or a realisation that is applied as its own adjoint),
  the quantum state of every register the run touched is restored
  exactly; otherwise to within the sum of the realisations' declared
  bounds (§12.3).
- **T5.** As §6.3.
- **T6 (executor agreement).** Two executors conforming to §3–§7 on one
  target with the same schedule produce on every x ∈ D the same final
  tuple for every oracle (exact) and, for every action with `operator_
  norm` inclusive bounds e₁, e₂, unitaries within e₁ + e₂ in operator
  norm; for channels, diamond bounds d₁ + d₂.

### 14.4 Mechanisation *(deferred)*

833:### 4.1 Classical control (CC, R)
844:| `load` / `store` | `r = load.iN addr` / `store.iN addr v` | N/8 bytes little-endian, unaligned; outside every live allocation: CC trap / R fault record. The suffix is always `.iN` with N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}: there is no pointer-typed load or store (a pointer is stored as `ptrtoint.iM` with M = ptr_width and reloaded with `inttoptr`) |
848:| `uncall` | `uncall @f (args) (results)` | **segment uncall**, legal exactly under §7.4: the most recent completed call segment of the history is a call of `@f` whose recorded boundary argument and result values equal the current operand values, every frame after it is pure, and it contains, at any nesting depth, no `measure` frame and no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified; otherwise CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP` with no state change beyond the fault record. The segment is reversed by the cancellation-aware history traversal of §7.4 (LIFO, nested segments included; frames already reversed and `uncall` markers skipped) and its frames are marked reversed; the `uncall` itself pushes a frame naming the segment |
