#[test]
fn context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported() {
    let mut r = request();
    let flip = "7ff7153a2ad3b857ec9eb86ca99b9bcbc605f60093d28e99fb749ed16d13274b";
    let signature = json!({"abi":"0.9","args":[],"bounds":[],"in":[],"kind":"perm_action","mem":[],"mut":[1,1],"tier":"q"});
    let function = "perm_action @pair (t:wire mut, u:wire mut) () () () ()\n^entry():\naction_call @flip (t) () () ()\naction_call @flip (u) () () ()";
    let hash = canonical::hash(
        format!(
            "isa=0.9\nptr_width=32\n{}\n{}\n{flip}\n",
            String::from_utf8(canonical::to_vec(&signature).unwrap()).unwrap(),
            function.replace("@flip", &format!("@{flip}"))
        )
        .as_bytes(),
    );
    let flip_signature = json!({"abi":"0.9","args":[],"bounds":[],"in":[],"kind":"perm_action","mem":[],"mut":[1],"tier":"q"});
    let symbols = json!({"0":{"name":"pair","kind":"perm_action","signature":signature,"interface_hash":hash},"1":{"name":"flip","kind":"perm_action","signature":flip_signature,"interface_hash":flip}});
    let source = format!(
        "bennett-object isa=0.9 object=0.9 launch=0.9 sched=0.9 cert=0.9 coverage=0.9 target=0.9 ptr_width=32\n\nsection symbols\n{}\n\nsection functions\n{function}\nperm_action @flip (t:wire mut) () () () ()\n^entry():\nx t\n",
        String::from_utf8(canonical::to_vec(&symbols).unwrap()).unwrap()
    );
    r.set_object(source.as_bytes()).unwrap();
    r.set_region(&hash, "forward", 0).unwrap();
    let mut target = r.target.as_ref().unwrap().value.clone();
    target["classes"] = json!({"A":{"capacity":1,"qubit_second":1,"dirty_rate":1},"B":{"capacity":1,"qubit_second":1,"dirty_rate":1}});
    target["gates"][0]["classes"] = json!(["*"]);
    target["gates"][0]["controls"] = json!({"min":0,"max":0});
    r.set_target(&canonical::to_vec(&target).unwrap()).unwrap();
    let response = r.schedule().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(response.outcome, Outcome::Unsupported);
    assert!(response.reason.to_str().unwrap().contains("frame"));
}

# v2v — frame descriptors for every realised invocation context

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-v2v` (P1).

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.1 “Invocation contexts” includes every reached invocation,
including defaults, with resolved arguments, budget and choices. §12.3
stores one region record per closure hash and requires one B5 frame.
§9.4 requires each slot's concrete target class and substitutes parameters
and budget into width constants before forming c-only expressions.
The same callee can receive A in one call and B in another; no single
descriptor can express both. A `(variant,controls)` key alone also fails
when those values agree. This is a C4/C5 contract defect.

North Star §§4.2/4.4/5.1 requires freely nested regions and independently
checkable schedules/certificates. Refusing repeated mixed-class calls
would narrow this machine. [4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) describes a different
case: a single slot fragmented within one context. Both repairs compose.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the source from `../bennett-sched/tests/pipeline.rs`, test
`context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported`:
root `pair(t,u)` calls the same one-bit `flip` on t and then u; flip is X.
Place t in A and u in B, capacity 1 each. Each call is forward with zero
inherited controls and the same closure hash. Callee frame one has
`mut:0,class:A,width:1`; callee frame two has B instead.

For all four root basis inputs, the result is `(t XOR 1,u XOR 1)`.
At unit duration/rate the root time is 2 and each root interface bit is
live for both slots: occupancy[A]=occupancy[B]=2, peaks 1 each.
The per-invocation callee views have duration 1 and just their respective
class. Neither copying A into both records nor taking a class wildcard
describes the realised placement. This witness needs no heterogeneous
slot and no nonzero epsilon.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Duplicate region records or rename the callee | Breaks closure-keyed identity or unnecessarily changes the source. |
| Use one wildcard/maximal frame | Cannot verify concrete classes and actual widths. |
| Exact context-indexed frames, uniform shorthand retained | Describes both calls and keeps old uniform certificates. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:

```text
frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
```

Append:

```text
Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
is admitted. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.

Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A standalone reference
record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
```

Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
and a width from another token budget at the same variant/control count.
Add a homogeneous two-call control accepted with old `frame`, and combine
one context with 4ta fragments. Finite output/resource arithmetic is
checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The sibling test was read, not run; its
current `Unsupported` expectation documents the gap, not a completed fix.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate-context repair**, coordinated with
[cwq](b-isa-spec-cwq.md) and [jw7](b-isa-spec-jw7.md). Keep v2v open until
the encoding and consumer tests are accepted. A 0.10 deferral must retain
this explicit blocker; it cannot restrict the admissible call graph.

Whether or not estimate is present, each reported value uses §10.1’s canonical 34-significant-digit upward decimal encoding. The checker compares that encoding with the encoding of its recomputed exact value, not the displayed decimal with the exact rational. When estimate is present, its reduced exact_resources fractions MUST also equal the recomputed values; K12’s existing mandatory-estimate obligations remain unchanged.' ## sol

Accept. The cited defect exists, and the proposed context records repair it without changing execution semantics or double-counting root costs. Hash and catalog claims hold for the proposed in-version repair. Remaining suggestions concern explanatory wording and executable fixtures.

**Path to accept:** Already acceptable. To raise C6 from 4 to 5, publish and execute the complete repeated-context certificate fixture with the stated positive totals and negative mutations.

- **C1 5**: The diff changes certificate metadata only. Invocation identity comes from §12.1; the machine state and §3.1 control invariant remain unchanged. *Change:* No required change. Optional §12.3 clarification: 'Resource-context records introduce no machine state or execution-time scheduling decision.'
- **C2 5**: No instruction changes. The worked action_call and ctl forms agree with §4.7, and native-leaf counting agrees with §§8.2–8.3. *Change:* No required change. Optional §12.3 clarification: 'gates_used counts native gate leaves after target-scheme expansion, each leaf exactly once.'

Oracle construction, cleanup and existing error composition are unchanged. *Change:* No required change. - **C5 4**: The defect is real: §12.1 identifies all scheduled contexts, while §12.3 supplies flat resource maps without selecting a context or aggregation. Full keys, canonical ordering, exact coverage and deterministic projection repair that contract without changing error-bound multiplicities. *Change:* §12.3, clarify the standalone boundary: 'Schedule-free reference claims retain K13's reference-only meaning; a verified standalone resource report binds an effective §11 schedule, including an explicit empty-entries schedule when defaults supply every decision.'
- **C6 4**: The checker obligation is stated and uses existing expansion and scheduling defaults. The proposed negative vectors are derivable. ## Blocking issues

- **[sol] FR-1** (Recommended exact spec diff, §12.3; interaction with §§5.3 and 14.1; contract): §12.3 requires verification.fault_rate in every region record, including action and perm_action. The diff unconditionally requires counts to be recomputed from F_B and applies its scheduled-context requirements to a 'scheduled region'. But §5.3 explicitly gives actions no fault outcome, and §14.1 defines F_B only for an oracle's reference body. Consequently the mandatory calculation is undefined for an otherwise valid action certificate. The published conformance/fixtures/controlled-action-09/controlled.bisa supplies a concrete witness: both controlled and flip are perm_action regions, neither invokes an oracle, and their invocation budget is zero. Filling in the proposed invocation fields does not create an F_B for either action. A checker must invent an exemption or fail to discharge a valid action certificate. *Fix:* In §12.3, change the schema to fault_rate: null | {invocation,domain,faults_by_code,undefined_excluded,layout_dependent_excluded}. State: 'The fault-rate recount and context requirements apply only to oracle records. For action and perm_action records, verification.fault_rate MUST be null and fault_rate_contexts MUST be absent. Actions have no fault outcome of their own (§5.3); each contained lifted-oracle invocation remains subject to the recount requirements in its corresponding oracle region record.' For the controlled-X fixture, both action records therefore contain fault_rate:null and no fault_rate_contexts. Alternatively retain action-shaped records, explicitly exempt them from F_B evaluation, and require faults_by_code:{} and both exclusion counts zero; I prefer null because it distinguishes inapplicability from a verified zero-fault oracle recount.
- **[codex] CFR-1** (Recommended exact spec diff: §12.3 fault_rate applicability; contract): *Fix:* In §12.3, make the field 'fault_rate: <oracle recount record> | null' and state: 'F_B recounts and fault_rate_contexts apply only to regions of kind oracle. For action and perm_action, fault_rate MUST be null and fault_rate_contexts MUST be absent. This denotes no action-owned fault outcome; it does not assert that nested oracle invocations are fault-free. Nested oracle recounts retain their own invocation/context bindings.' The corrected controlled-action fixture has fault_rate:null on both perm_action records. Alternatively, retain an explicitly defined empty action placeholder that is never evaluated through F_B and never discharges a callee obligation; I recommend null because it distinguishes inapplicability from a verified zero-fault recount.

## sol

The invocation defect is real, the 256-tuple witness is correct, and the hash/catalog claims hold for the proposed in-version repair. One contract blocker remains: the unconditional recount rule invokes oracle-only F_B semantics for required action records.

**Path to accept:** Scope F_B recounts and their context requirements to oracle records, and define the required action field explicitly using the null rule in FR-1 or its stated empty-record alternative. No core semantic change is needed.

- **C1 5**: The proposal changes certificate metadata, leaving the accepted machine state and step relation unchanged. *Change:* None required in §§3–5; retain the existing machine model.
- **C2 5**: §4.2 charges one step per lifted row, and §14.1 makes retval terminate successfully. The two-row witness therefore faults at B=1 and succeeds at B=2. *Change:* §15, optional fixture rule: 'The add/retval oracle with a=1 reports 256 code-1 faults at B=1 and no faults at B=2 over the full eight-bit interface domain.'
- **C3 5**: Counting code(F_B), rather than the accumulated fault word, correctly follows §5.2. Incoming fault word 1 becomes 0 on code-1 failure and remains 1 on success. *Change:* §12.3, optional explicit predicate: 'A domain tuple contributes to faults_by_code[c] exactly when code(F_B(x))=c and c≠0.'
- **C4 5**: Explicit budgets and resolved context bindings preserve §5.4 token semantics without changing target matching or resource accounting. *Change:* None required in §5.4; retain the rule that each oracle invocation uses its supplied token's tuple.
- **C5 4**: The defect is real: §12.3 binds individual claims and scheduled roots but does not bind fault_rate. The proposed repair selects oracle invocations deterministically. Its universal F_B recount requirement, however, also reaches required action records, for which F_B is undefined. *Change:* §12.3: 'Fault-rate recount and context requirements apply only to oracle records. For action and perm_action records, verification.fault_rate MUST be null and fault_rate_contexts MUST be absent.' Update the schema accordingly.
- **C6 4**: The oracle vectors are correct. The verification-index script passes; independent replay using the available ISA-0.8 binary confirms the unchanged arithmetic and boundary semantics. This does not establish complete 0.9 recount implementation. An action applicability vector is missing. *Change:* §15: 'The controlled-X action fixture has fault_rate:null without evaluating F_B; any contained oracle invocations retain independently checked fault-rate records.'
- **C7 4**: The repair preserves North Star §5.1 reporting and §8 zero-fault gates for exact oracle invocations. The blanket recount wording needs qualification to preserve certificates for the quantum actions promised by North Star §4.2. *Change:* §12.3: 'An action's lack of its own fault outcome does not discharge fault-rate obligations for its lifted-oracle callees.'
- **C8 4**: K13 encodings, canonical context order, first-context projection and rejection of unbound verified counts give implementers a practical migration rule. §9.2 confirms that certificate changes leave object_hash, symbol envelopes and schedule_hash unchanged; the published 219-recipe catalog check also passes. *Change:* §12.3: 'For action and perm_action records, null denotes that no action-level fault outcome exists; it is distinct from an oracle recount reporting zero faults.'

Suggestions:
1. §12.3: explicitly state, 'Fault-rate domains use §12.2 component resolution, validity, omission-at-zero and enumeration rules; each enumerated interface tuple contributes separately.' Thus the worked full domain contains 256 tuples, rather than only four distinct oracle inputs.
2. §12.3: state, 'If host limits prevent completion of a legal recount, report unchecked (host limit), discharging no verified fault-rate obligation; do not reject the domain or substitute smaller counts.' This extends §12.2's existing host-limit treatment directly to the new recount operation.
3. In the supporting exclusion wording, identify North Star §§3.2 and 5.1 as the inherited obligation: §12.3 itself presently only declares the exclusion counters. Suggested sentence: 'Exclusion counts require independently validated classifications under the recorded materialisation and classification configuration; unavailable validation leaves the recount unchecked and cannot justify an assumed exclusion.'

The central defect, clause references, two-budget witness and unchanged non-certificate hash claims check out. The proposal needs two contract corrections: action applicability and its unsupported assumption about exclusion validation. No core semantic change is needed.

- **C4 5**: No resource or target semantics change. Under an authorized in-version K repair, §9.2 excludes certificate metadata from object_hash and symbol envelopes; schedule, target and catalog bytes remain unchanged. The catalog check reproduced hash 5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197. *Change:* No resource change required; retain the note's certificate-byte migration requirement and unchanged object, closure, target, schedule and catalog bindings.
- **C5 3**: The invocation-binding defect is real: §12.3 binds standalone claims individually but supplies no corresponding fault_rate binding. """Generate the complete draft catalog or historical family checkpoints. Python -I, stdlib only.
No interpreter imports. Does not publish or update the normative hash marker.
"""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import runpy
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
def jcs(v):
    # This catalog uses integer numbers below 2**53; no floats. UTF-16 key order.
    if type(v) is bool: return "true" if v else "false"
    if isinstance(v, dict):
        return '{' + ','.join(jcs(k)+':'+jcs(v[k]) for k in sorted(v, key=lambda k:k.encode('utf-16be'))) + '}'
    if isinstance(v, list):
        return '[' + ','.join(map(jcs,v)) + ']'
    if isinstance(v, str):
        return json.dumps(v, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(',', ':'))
    if type(v) is int and abs(v) < 2**53:
        return str(v)
    raise ValueError(f'outside catalog JCS subset: {v!r}')
def plus(a,b): return ['+',a,b]
def minus(a,b): return ['-',a,b]
def times(a,b): return ['*',a,b]
def wire(name, bit=0): return name+'['+jcs(bit)+']'
def gate(g,*w): return {'gate':g,'wires':list(w)}
def alloc(n,w): return {'alloc':n,'width':w,'class':'any','state':'clean'}
def release(n): return {'release':n}
def loop(i,n,b): return {'for':i,'from':0,'to':n,'body':b}
def inv(b): return {'inverse':True,'body':b}
class Circuit:
    def __init__(self): self.body=[]; self.scratch=[]; self.serial=0
    def temp(self,width=1):
        n=f'z{self.serial}'; self.serial+=1
        self.scratch.append(n); self.body.append(alloc(n,width)); return n
    def bit(self): return wire(self.temp())
    def boolean(self,e,out):
        """Postorder tree, fresh scratch per node, no CSE; root XOR out."""
        computes=[]; names=[]
        def visit(e):
            if isinstance(e,str): return e
            op,*args=e
            if op=='or': return visit(('xor',('xor',*args),('and',*args)))
            ws=[visit(a) for a in args]
            n=self.temp(); names.append(n); d=wire(n)
            if op=='not': ops=[gate('x',d),gate('cx',ws[0],d)]
            elif op=='xor': ops=[gate('cx',s,d) for s in ws]
            elif op=='and': ops=[gate('cx',ws[0],d)] if ws[0]==ws[1] else [gate('ccx',*ws,d)]
            else: raise ValueError(op)
            self.body.extend(ops); computes.extend(ops); return d
        root=visit(e); self.body.append(gate('cx',root,out)); self.body.append(inv(computes))
        self.body.extend(release(n) for n in reversed(names))
    def ripple(self,a,b,n,sub=False):
        p=self.temp(n); s=self.temp(n); c=self.temp(plus(n,1))
        body_start=len(self.body)
        if sub: self.body.append(gate('x',wire(c)))
        start=len(self.body)
        aa=a('i'); bb=b('i')
        self.boolean(('xor',aa,('not',bb) if sub else bb),wire(p,'i'))
        self.boolean(('xor',wire(p,'i'),wire(c,'i')),wire(s,'i'))
        self.boolean(('xor',('and',aa,('not',bb) if sub else bb),('and',wire(p,'i'),wire(c,'i'))),wire(c,plus('i',1)))
        bitbody=self.body[start:]; self.body[start:]=[loop('i',n,bitbody)]
        return s,c,body_start
    def finish(self,outops,start=0):
        compute=self.body[start:]; self.body=self.body[:start]+compute+outops+[inv(compute)]
    def recipe(self,kind,params,source):
        return {'kind':kind,'parameters':params,'body':self.body,'scratch':self.scratch,'removable':[], 'source':source}
def recipe(kind,params,body,source,scratch=()):
    return {'kind':kind,'parameters':params,'body':body,'scratch':list(scratch),'removable':[],'source':source}
def copy_family():
    out=[]
    for k in ['qxor','qcopy','quncopy','qexch']:
        op=gate('swap',wire('a','i'),wire('b','i')) if k=='qexch' else gate('cx',wire('s','i'),wire('t','i'))
        body=[loop('i','N',[op])]
        if k=='qcopy': body.insert(0,alloc('t','N'))
        if k=='quncopy': body += [inv([alloc('t','N')])]
        out.append(recipe(k,['N'],body,'0.8 §4.5: '+('swap a[i] b[i]' if k=='qexch' else 'cx s[i] t[i]')))
    for k,ws in [('x',['a']),('cx',['a','b']),('ccx',['a','b','t']),('swap',['a','b'])]:
        out.append(recipe('gate.'+k,[],[gate(k,*map(wire,ws))],'0.8 §4.4: '+k+' is exact, including global phase.'))
    return out
def arithmetic_family():
    out=[]
    for op in ['add','sub']:
        c=Circuit(); s,carry,_=c.ripple(lambda i:wire('a',i),lambda i:wire('b',i),'N',op=='sub')
        compute=c.body[:]
        c.body += [loop('j','N',[gate('cx',wire(s,'j'),wire('t','j'))]),inv(compute)]
        out.append(c.recipe('value.'+op,['N'],'0.8 §4.1: add sub wrap; §17 item 7: ripple-carry adders and comparators.'))
    for op in ['eq','ne','ult','ule','slt','sle']:
        c=Circuit();carry=None;eq=None
        if op in ['ult','ule','slt','sle']: _,carry,_=c.ripple(lambda i:wire('a',i),lambda i:wire('b',i),'N',True)
        if op in ['eq','ne','ule','sle']:
            eq=c.temp(plus('N',1)); c.body.append(gate('x',wire(eq)))
            start=len(c.body)
            c.boolean(('and',wire(eq,'i'),('not',('xor',wire('a','i'),wire('b','i')))),wire(eq,plus('i',1)))
            c.body[start:]=[loop('i','N',c.body[start:])]
        u=('not',wire(carry,'N')) if carry else None; e=wire(eq,'N') if eq else None
        v={'eq':e,'ne':('not',e),'ult':u,'ule':('or',u,e)}.get(op)
        if v is None:
            sa=wire('a',minus('N',1)); sb=wire('b',minus('N',1)); diff=('xor',sa,sb)
            signed=('xor',('and',diff,sa),('and',('not',diff),u))
            v=signed if op=='slt' else ('or',signed,e)
        dest=c.temp(); c.boolean(v,wire(dest)); compute=c.body[:]
        c.body += [gate('cx',wire(dest),wire('t')),inv(compute)]
        out.append(c.recipe('value.icmp.'+op,['N'],'0.8 §4.1: icmp.{eq,ne,ult,ule,slt,sle}; §17 item 7: ripple-carry adders and comparators.'))
    return out

def build(families):
    recipes=copy_family()
    if families>=2: recipes+=arithmetic_family()

## 5. Regions

### 5.1 Interfaces and common well-formedness

Signatures list roles: `oracle @o (in a: wire[8]) (tgt t: wire[8]) (fault
f: wire[4]) (mem db: region[10,8] in) (args n: i64)`. **Interface
regions** are caller-owned, passed by name, role `in` (returned to entry
contents on every basis configuration) or `mut` (actions only). In the
text form (§16.2) a wire param's role suffix is optional and, when
present, MUST equal its group's role (`in` in an oracle's or action's
`in` group, `mut` in an action's `mut` group); a region param always
carries its role (`in` or `mut`); other region-interface params carry
no suffix. Lproc parameter roles are specified in §3.7 and §16.2. A region
MUST NOT contain `measure effect br call uncall trap hostcall qreg.* budget.mint` or
classical memory instructions, nor lifted rows outside oracle bodies.
Predicate stack empty at entry and exit. Every `qalloc` matched by a
`qfree` of a clean bundle, every `qborrow` by a `qreturn` after its
frame, every local `qregion` restored, by **explicit cleanup
instructions**; the schedule chooses only realisations. **`in` wires in
actions** appear only as controls (including the address operands of
`qload qunload qstore qexchm`, the wires pushed by `qpred`, the selector
of `qif`, whose two `x` gates restore it, and the `cond` wires of `qloop …
while`), as targets of diagonal gates, as `qcopy`/`qxor` sources, as the
value operand of `qstore` (it is only read, as the control of the XOR
into the selected cell), or as `in` arguments of calls. The **region
call graph** — an edge from a function to the callee of each of its
`oracle_call`, `action_call`, `lift.oracle_call`, `lift.oracle_uncall`
and `lift.perm_call` instructions and to both callees of each
`qinplace`, an oracle counting the rows of its lproc closure as its own —
is acyclic (suite F); recursion exists only through `lift.call` among
lprocs.

### 5.2 Lifted oracle

**Body**: lifted and control rows over its locations and lproc closure;
**exact-permutation only** (ε = 0 everywhere, transitively).

**Contract** on every x ∈ D: `ins` unchanged; `tgt` final = initial ⊕
payload(F_B(x)); `fr` final = initial ⊕ code(F_B(x)); `in` regions
unchanged; borrowed bundles returned (§5.6); workspace, locations, local
regions, step state clean / at image. **F_B(x)** = `(f(x), 0)` if the
reference interpreter reaches `lift.retval` within B steps without a
fault (f(x) = the XOR-accumulated `result_val`), else `(0, code)` with
`code` the first fault in sequential order, or `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` if
not halted after B steps. The oracle is an **involution** on `(ins, tgt,
fr)` and its own adjoint.

**Construction**: (1) allocate step state (`result_val` included),
locations, local regions, `alloctab` pre-entries, B frames; (2) run B
forward steps; (3) **commit**: `at_HALT = (pc == HALT)`; under `at_HALT ∧
¬faulted`: `qxor result_val tgt`; under `faulted`: `qxor code fr`; under
`¬at_HALT`: XOR the constant `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` into `fr`; uncompute
`at_HALT`; (4) **prefix reversal**: run the inverse step for t = B..1,
then release (1). The commit writes persist.

**Domain D**: every value of each `ins`, of `tgt`, of `fr`, of every cell
of every `in` region, of every borrowed bundle; `qalloc`ed wires at 0;
local regions at image.

### 5.3 Quantum action

**Semantics.** The body denotes a unitary U on mut ⊗ ins ⊗ mem ⊗
(borrowed), where mem includes both `mut` and `in` interface regions,
block-diagonal in the computational basis of `ins` and of `in`-region
cells, identity on borrowed and external reference systems (§5.6),
workspace returned clean, with domain D every value of each `mut`
and `in` bundle, every cell of every `mut` and `in` interface region and
every borrowed bundle, workspace initially at 0 and local regions at
image. [deps: ISA-5.1-P1, ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-5.6-P1, ISA-14.2] No fault outcome. Coherent internal
control yields the padded unitary under declared bounds, part of the
action's identity. **Permutation action**: realised unitary a
permutation matrix (ε = 0, permutation gates only, transitively).

**Body**: gates; `qcopy quncopy qxor qexch qinplace`; `qload qunload
qstore qexchm`; `qpred qunpred qif qloop`; `oracle_call action_call`;
`qalloc qborrow qfree qreturn`; `qregion`.
An action body with several `block`s executes its blocks in text order,
each a unitary fragment; labels are descriptive and carry no control
semantics, and lifetimes may span blocks.

**Bounds.** The `bounds { … }` list of an action's `regs` line and the
`bounds` array of its signature hold, in text order, the trip count `n`
of every `qloop` and `qloop … while` in the body (nested loops
included, outer before inner); the list MUST equal the body (suite F)
and is empty for a body without loops. It is part of the action's
identity because the padded unitary of coherent internal control
depends on it.

**Adjoint table** (reverse order, instruction replaced, versions
renumbered):

```
  rx/ry/rz(θ) ↦ (−θ)   s ↔ sdg   t ↔ tdg   x cx ccx mcx swap h cz: self-adjoint
  qalloc ↔ qfree   qborrow ↔ qreturn   qregion creation ↔ release   qcopy ↔ quncopy   qload ↔ qunload
  qxor qexch qstore qexchm: self-adjoint   qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins) (mem) (args) ↦ qinplace @ginv @g tk x (ins) (mem) (args)
  oracle_call ↦ oracle_call
  action_call @b ↦ adj action_call @b   qpred/qunpred pairs reversed as a whole
  qif w {A}{B} ↦ adjoint of the complete §4.6 expansion, including unconditional lifetimes, in reverse order. [deps: ISA-3.5, ISA-4.6-P7]
  qloop n {A} ↦ qloop n {adj A}
  qloop n while cond {A} ↦ flags computed forward as §4.6; adj A for i = n..1 under c_i; flags uncomputed
```

**Control.** `ctl w @a` is controlled-U: workspace, regions and flags
allocated unconditionally; every gate of the body and its cleanup runs
under `w` in addition to the active predicate.

The shorthand `qif w {adj A}{adj B}` denotes that adjoint when each
arm's allocations and releases are matched within that arm; no
arm-local lifetime restriction is imposed on the source `qif`.
[deps: ISA-3.5, ISA-4.6-P7, ISA-5.1]

Two operations form a **matched pair** iff the second is the §5.3
adjoint of the first, with the same callee(s) (the pair exchanged for
`qinplace`), identical classical argument values and token identities
and tuples, the same operand wires with the first's read-only input
versions and produced target versions current at the second, and equal
effective control sets including the versions of their wires; order and
repetition in predicate stacks are irrelevant. [deps: ISA-2-P10, ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.3-P4, ISA-5.4]
A matched inverse restores preceding wire-version bindings for
subsequent matching only when the §6.2 cleanup conditions hold; matching
alone does not discharge an outstanding-reader or phase obligation, and
a cell write always advances its cell version even when its value is
restored. [deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-6.1-P1, ISA-6.2]

Scheduled action adjoints use the source replacements above before
reference-recipe instantiation, with the ordinal contract and `adj`
adapter specified in §11.1 (K62). [deps: ISA-5.3-P4, ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P29]

fn frame09(value: &Json, f: &Function, contexts: &[u128]) -> Result<()> {
    let mut ids = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut frames = BTreeSet::new();
    for group in ["slots", "scratch", "history", "borrow", "ancillae"] {
        for record in value.get(group)?.array()? {
            let id = record.get("id")?.string()?;
            if !ids.insert(id) {
                return err("§9.4", "duplicate frame identity");
            }
            if group != "slots" {
                frames.insert(id);
            }
            for c in contexts {
                frame_width(record.get("width")?, *c)?;
            }
        }
    }
    let mut expected = Vec::new();
    let groups: &[(&str, usize)] = match f.kind.as_str() {
        "oracle" => &[("in", 0), ("tgt", 1), ("fault", 2), ("mem", 3)],
        "action" | "perm_action" => &[("mut", 0), ("in", 1), ("mem", 2)],
        _ => &[],
    };
    for (group, index) in groups {
        for (i, p) in f.params[*index].iter().enumerate() {
            let (role, width) = match p.ty {
                Type::Wire(w) => ((*group).to_string(), w as u128),
                Type::Region(a, w) => (
                    format!("mem_{}", p.role.as_deref().unwrap_or("in")),
                    crate::oracle::power_of_two(a as u128)
                        .and_then(|n| n.checked_mul(w as u128))
                        .ok_or_else(|| {
                            crate::resource_error("§9.4", "interface region width exceeds u128")
                        })?,
                ),
                _ => return err("§9.4", "invalid frame interface type"),
            };
            expected.push((format!("{group}:{i}"), role, width));
        }
    }
    let slots = value.get("slots")?.array()?;
    if slots.len() != expected.len() {
        return err("§9.4", "frame interface slot count mismatch");
    }
    for (slot, (id, role, width)) in slots.iter().zip(expected) {
        if slot.get("id")?.string()? != id || slot.get("role")?.string()? != role || width == 0 {
            return err("§9.4", "frame interface identity or role mismatch");
        }
        for c in contexts {
            if frame_width(slot.get("width")?, *c)? != width {
                return err("§9.4", "frame interface width mismatch");
            }
        }
    }
    if let Some(ranges) = value.object()?.get("live_ranges") {
        let mut prior = BTreeMap::<&str, Vec<(u128, u128)>>::new();
        for r in ranges.array()? {
            let id = r.get("frame")?.string()?;
            let (lo, hi) = (r.get("start")?.uint()?, r.get("end")?.uint()?);
            if !frames.contains(id)
                || lo >= hi
                || prior
                    .entry(id)
                    .or_default()
                    .iter()
                    .any(|(a, b)| lo < *b && *a < hi)
            {
                return err("§9.4", "invalid or overlapping non-slot frame live range");
            }
            prior.entry(id).or_default().push((lo, hi));
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn integer_json(n: u64) -> Value {
    if n >= 1 << 53 {
        json!(n.to_string())
    } else {
        json!(n)
    }
}
fn slot_record(slot: &Slot, allocator: &Allocator<'_>) -> Result<Value> {
    let class = allocator
        .allocations
        .iter()
        .find(|a| slot.wires.first().is_some_and(|w| a.wires.contains(w)))
        .map(|a| a.class.as_str())
        .ok_or_else(|| Error::infeasible("interface slot has no recorded class"))?;
    Ok(json!({"id":slot.id,"role":slot.role,"class":class,"width":slot.wires.len()}))
}

def recipe(kind,params,body,source,scratch=()):
    return {'kind':kind,'parameters':params,'body':body,'scratch':list(scratch),'removable':[],'source':source}
def copy_family():
    out=[]
    for k in ['qxor','qcopy','quncopy','qexch']:
        op=gate('swap',wire('a','i'),wire('b','i')) if k=='qexch' else gate('cx',wire('s','i'),wire('t','i'))
        body=[loop('i','N',[op])]
        if k=='qcopy': body.insert(0,alloc('t','N'))
        if k=='quncopy': body += [inv([alloc('t','N')])]
        out.append(recipe(k,['N'],body,'0.8 §4.5: '+('swap a[i] b[i]' if k=='qexch' else 'cx s[i] t[i]')))
    for k,ws in [('x',['a']),('cx',['a','b']),('ccx',['a','b','t']),('swap',['a','b'])]:
        out.append(recipe('gate.'+k,[],[gate(k,*map(wire,ws))],'0.8 §4.4: '+k+' is exact, including global phase.'))
    return out
def arithmetic_family():
    out=[]
    for op in ['add','sub']:
        c=Circuit(); s,carry,_=c.ripple(lambda i:wire('a',i),lambda i:wire('b',i),'N',op=='sub')
        compute=c.body[:]
        c.body += [loop('j','N',[gate('cx',wire(s,'j'),wire('t','j'))]),inv(compute)]
        out.append(c.recipe('value.'+op,['N'],'0.8 §4.1: add sub wrap; §17 item 7: ripple-carry adders and comparators.'))
    for op in ['eq','ne','ult','ule','slt','sle']:
        c=Circuit();carry=None;eq=None
        if op in ['ult','ule','slt','sle']: _,carry,_=c.ripple(lambda i:wire('a',i),lambda i:wire('b',i),'N',True)
        if op in ['eq','ne','ule','sle']:
            eq=c.temp(plus('N',1)); c.body.append(gate('x',wire(eq)))
            start=len(c.body)
            c.boolean(('and',wire(eq,'i'),('not',('xor',wire('a','i'),wire('b','i')))),wire(eq,plus('i',1)))
            c.body[start:]=[loop('i','N',c.body[start:])]
        u=('not',wire(carry,'N')) if carry else None; e=wire(eq,'N') if eq else None
        v={'eq':e,'ne':('not',e),'ult':u,'ule':('or',u,e)}.get(op)
        if v is None:
            sa=wire('a',minus('N',1)); sb=wire('b',minus('N',1)); diff=('xor',sa,sb)
            signed=('xor',('and',diff,sa),('and',('not',diff),u))
            v=signed if op=='slt' else ('or',signed,e)
        dest=c.temp(); c.boolean(v,wire(dest)); compute=c.body[:]
        c.body += [gate('cx',wire(dest),wire('t')),inv(compute)]
        out.append(c.recipe('value.icmp.'+op,['N'],'0.8 §4.1: icmp.{eq,ne,ult,ule,slt,sle}; §17 item 7: ripple-carry adders and comparators.'))
    return out

def build(families):
    recipes=copy_family()
    if families>=2: recipes+=arithmetic_family()
    if families>=3: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_arithmetic.py'))['family'](globals())

    if families>=17: recipes+=runpy.run_path(str(ROOT/'conformance/tools/ref_qinplace.py'))['family'](globals())
    recipes.sort(key=lambda r:r['kind'].encode())
    return {'isa':'0.9','name':'ref','version':'0.9','recipes':recipes}

preserves all bindings and emits two distinct invocation paths starting

**Oracle invocation context (K77).** An oracle's catalog argument vector
contains exactly its declared parameters, flattened in header-group order
`in`, `tgt`, `fault`, `mem`, `args` (§16.2); empty groups add no entries.
The invocation token is not an additional formal or vector entry.
Before concrete expansion, consumers MUST supply and validate the
explicit source-binding channel `oracle_invocation`, preserve it through
the captures below, and reject missing, ill-typed or source-inconsistent
context. The channel is a record with exactly one field, `token`, of
K24's `value` type with `isa_type:"token"`: four canonical nonnegative
decimal strings in §5.4 order `(B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit)`,
resolved from that source call's `tk` and
validated for the linked callee under §5.4. A root oracle instead binds
the explicit launch/schedule request budget. Unresolved source parameters
retain §11's admission/concrete-check staging.

This channel is explicit input to the linked source-body builder, outside
the catalog JSON and positional actuals, not an implicit name, default,
new constructor key or token arithmetic inside a recipe. The registry
binds the closure's source/interface; its builder instantiates the
source-derived constructor body separately for each invocation, using
this tuple and the linked source/layout to derive B, frames and every
token-dependent limit or width supplied to `oracle.construct`.
A registry keyed by closure hash alone cannot fix one invocation budget.
The context is associated with the full §11 invocation identity, not
with the closure hash or positional vector values alone.

Source binding captures the channel with the corresponding `arguments`
actual and with any body/template adapter containing that call. Forwarding
an arguments name through `invoke` or `call`, or splicing/applying a
captured body/template, preserves that captured channel; unrelated
caller/apply-site context cannot replace it. The qinplace adapters capture
the source `tk` for both calls. Each nested source oracle call resolves
its own `tk` in its own fixed bindings, then restores the outer context.
Source-adjoint adapters retain their captured tuples and perform §5.3
replacements before recipe instantiation (K62). Inverse traversal retains
each instantiated forward call's channel and forward occurrence identity
while reversing/adjointing its operations (§11). None of these captures
adds a leaf, ordinal, source parameter or closure-hash component.

**Two-token witness.** Use §16.3 example 1's `inc8` closure and two source
calls `oracle_call @inc8 tk2 (x) t f () ()` and
`oracle_call @inc8 tk3 (x) t f () ()` in one action expansion, with
`tk2=(2,0,0,0)` and `tk3=(3,0,0,0)`. Both positional vectors are `[x,t,f]`;
their captured contexts are respectively `{"token":["2","0","0","0"]}`
and `{"token":["3","0","0","0"]}`. The linked closure hash is identical.
The first construction has two frames and XORs `(0,FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS)`
into `(t,f)`; the second has three frames and XORs `(x+1 mod 256,0)`.
Starting from `(t,f)=(0,0)`, the final pair is `(x+1 mod 256,1)`.
Their §11 edges are distinct: separate source points each have
`call_index=0`; two calls within a single source-row forwarding expansion
instead have forward `call_index=0,1`. Inverse traversal retains those
identities and tuples and restores the complete input. Neither binding
adds a token to `inc8`'s empty signature `args` list or changes its hash.

`type`, `fields`, `order`. `fields` is an ordered typed formal list;

 succeeded in 136ms:
logarithm. The existing out-of-array rule applies to this integer. An
entry with `epsilon` 0 MUST NOT use `eps_decade`, including through a
table lookup. This extends the exact-entry prohibition above; it does
not assign an index to eps = 0. Motivation (0.8 §8.1): the grammar names
`"index": "k" | "eps_decade"`, and "Division by zero and a table index
outside the array are load-time errors"; 0.8 defines no decimal index.

**EPS-DECADE witness**

```json
[{"eps":"1","index":0},{"eps":"0.1","index":1},{"eps":"0.1001","index":1},{"eps":"0.0999","index":2},{"eps":"0.001","index":3},{"eps":"0.000999","index":4}]
```

For eps = 999/10000, eps·10 = 999/1000 < 1 and eps·100 = 999/100 ≥ 1,
so the index is 2. A two-entry table rejects this index, a three-entry
table selects element 2. eps = 0, eps > 1 and an unbound eps reject;
no clamping, padding or fallback entry is admitted.

**Variable binding** by evaluation context; referencing a variable that
the context does not bind is a load-time error:

| Context | Bound variables |
|---|---|
| a `gates` entry's `epsilon` and `cost` | `k` = the gate's own control count; `c` = its total control count (`k` + effective control count, §8.3); `eps` = the operation's `epsilon_share` from the schedule (§11) |
| a scheme's `ancillae.count`, template wire subscripts and `repeat` bounds | `k`, `c` as above; `i` inside a `repeat`; `theta` for a parametric scheme |
| a template gate's `params` | `theta`, `i` |
| `qram` costs | `a` = the region's address width A; `w` = its cell width W; `c` |
| a frame descriptor (§9.4) | `c` only |
| `classes.*`, `capabilities.*`, `resources.*.rate` | none (numbers only) |

**Requested versus achieved accuracy.** `eps` is the `epsilon_share`
the schedule assigns to the operation (§11; the default share is the
region's `epsilon` divided by its number of ε > 0 operations). A `gates`
entry whose `epsilon` expression evaluates to a value > `eps` does not
match the operation and matching continues with the next entry; the
error charged to a matched operation is the entry's evaluated `epsilon`;
an entry with `epsilon` 0 is exact and matches every `eps`.

    43	
    44	## Recommended exact spec diff
    45	
    46	Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:
    47	
    48	```text
    49	frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
    50	frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
    51	```
    52	
    53	Append:
    54	
    55	```text
    56	Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
    57	frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
    58	of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
    59	each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
    60	resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
    61	frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
    62	in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
    63	key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
    64	variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
    65	is admitted. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
    66	controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
    67	bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.
    68	
    69	Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
    70	slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
    71	that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
    72	from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
    73	identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
    74	descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
    75	live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A standalone reference
    76	record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
    77	proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
    78	```
    79	
    80	Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
    81	fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
    82	reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
    83	The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
    84	consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.
    85	
    86	## Hashes, catalog and consumers
    87	
    88	No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
    89	required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
    90	the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
    91	specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
    92	default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
    93	the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
    94	the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.
    95	
    96	## Conformance vectors and verification
    97	
    98	Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
    99	then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
   100	duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
   101	and a width from another token budget at the same variant/control count.
   102	Add a homogeneous two-call control accepted with old `frame`, and combine
   103	one context with 4ta fragments. Finite output/resource arithmetic is
   104	checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The sibling test was read, not run; its
   105	current `Unsupported` expectation documents the gap, not a completed fix.
   106	
   107	## Release and route
   108	
   109	Recommend a **0.9 K certificate-context repair**, coordinated with
   110	[cwq](b-isa-spec-cwq.md) and [jw7](b-isa-spec-jw7.md). Keep v2v open until
   111	the encoding and consumer tests are accepted. A 0.10 deferral must retain
   112	this explicit blocker; it cannot restrict the admissible call graph.
